From Holst to Carroll Gravity, a Hamiltonian point of view
J. Fernando Barbero G, Juan Margalef-Bentabol, Valle Varo, Eduardo J.S. Villaseñor

Abstract
The Carrollian regime of gravity provides a useful ultrarelativistic framework for studying asymptotically flat spacetimes, horizon dynamics, and condensed matter systems. In this paper, we present a comprehensive Hamiltonian analysis of the most general Carroll-invariant Lagrangian that can be derived from the Holst action. To guide the analysis, Cartan geometry tools are used. We identify the full constraint structure of the theory, characterize its gauge symmetries, obtain the explicit form of the Hamiltonian vector fields and introduce Ashtekar-like variables for the magnetic Carrollian regime. We also discuss in detail an analog of the time gauge employed in the Hamiltonian analysis of the Holst action for general relativity.



June 30, 2026

Key Words:

Carrollian gravity; Group contractions; Gravitational actions.

1
Introduction

The Carrollian limit of gravitational theories provides an interesting setting to study a number of problems related to general relativity (GR) and cosmology such as the study of black hole horizons
[
1
]
and asymptotic null infinity
[
2
]
. It also finds interesting applications in other parts of physics
[
3
]
. Many of these applications take advantage of the fact that in the Carrollian limit light cones degenerate into curves and, hence, the nature of causality in Carrollian spacetimes is somewhat simplified (although, arguably, it is changed in a rather non-trivial fashion). From a mathematical perspective, the main idea in the Carrollian setting is to introduce a contraction of the Poincaré algebra within the gravitational models at hand along the lines first proposed by Lévy-Leblond
[
4
]
and Sen Gupta
[
5
]
. In recent years there has been a renewed interest to understand the intricacies of the dynamics of the electric and magnetic sectors of Carrollian theories
[
6
]
and extend their applications (see recent papers, such as
[
8
,
7
,
9
,
10
,
11
]
, for comprehensive lists of references).

The purpose of this paper is to study in detail the Hamiltonian formulation for the most general Carrollian gravitational Lagrangian derivable from the Holst action
[
12
]
for GR. This Lagrangian is the sum of the Carrollian gauge-invariant 4-forms given in
[
13
]
(see also
[
14
]
). One application of the results that we present here is to provide relevant toy models for the canonical quantization of gravity. In fact, for the action discussed in this work, we will find that the phase space is related to the one corresponding to the Ashtekar formulation for GR
[
15
,
16
]
. The only significant difference between the Hamiltonian formulation presented here and the standard Hamiltonian formulation for Lorentzian gravity in terms of real Ashtekar variables is the form of the Hamiltonian constraint: whereas in the case of GR this constraint can be written as the combination of two terms, one involving the curvature of the Ashtekar connection and the other the curvature of the spin connection built with the triads, in the Carrollian case only the last term is present. This is a simplification of sorts that may enable the canonical quantization of the action discussed here.

We would like to point out that the present work is not the first one to study the Hamiltonian formulation of a Carrollian gravity. For example, the equivalence between the magnetic Carrollian limit of Einstein gravity and the Carrollian theory of gravity was established in
[
6
]
by using Hamiltonian techniques. Also, in
[
17
]
the author performs a Hamiltonian analysis of the Carrollian limit of the Cartan-Palatini action for GR the action discussed in
[
14
]
by following a line of thought similar to the one presented in
[
18
]
(in particular, relying on the use of a quadratic constraint) and taking a limit. There are several well-established methods to deal with singular (constrained) Lagrangian systems and obtain the corresponding Hamiltonian formulations. The best known of them is due to Dirac
[
19
]
. If followed to the letter, it always gives a Hamiltonian picture of dynamics equivalent to the one given from the action through the Euler-Lagrange equations. However, the implementation of the procedure is often tricky, in particular, the resolution of the equations that determine the undetermined multipliers introduced in the definition of the total Hamiltonian. If these equations are not solved, it is possible to miss constraints and arrive at incorrect results. A nagging feature of the approach followed in
[
18
]
is the substitution of primary constraints linear in momenta by a quadratic one which is then used to define the total Hamiltonian. The reason for this is rather obscure but is probably justified by the appearance of dynamical objects in the primary constraints that are interpreted as Lagrange multipliers and, for this reason, considered (incorrectly) as non-dynamical objects. Another unnatural feature of the mentioned approach is that, instead of working with the natural variables in which the Cartan-Palatini action is written (a coframe and an spin connection) it uses a frame field. This unnecessarily complicates the form of the action and makes all the computations significantly harder.

The approach we follow here is different in spirit on several grounds. First, because we consider the most general Carrollian Lagrangian derivable from the Holst action (see
[
13
]
), including a term with the Immirzi parameter. Second, from a methodological standpoint, we follow the clean and geometrically inspired approach developed by Gotay, Nester and Hinds (GNH) in
[
20
,
21
]
to study constrained systems. This method provides an alternative to Dirac’s approach, while yielding essentially equivalent information. Finally, it offers several computational and conceptual advantages.

To complete the analysis of the relevant equations in the GNH setting (and, likewise, in Dirac’s formalism), it is often useful to exploit geometric insights that clarify the structure of their solutions. In particular, to solve the equations that determine the components of the Hamiltonian vector fields, brute-force computations can always be used. However, such an approach frequently obscures the underlying structure of the solutions, making their interpretation difficult. It can also be challenging to verify the geometric consistency conditions that these vector fields must satisfy. A preliminary geometric analysis, together with the identification of the relevant symmetries, provides a clearer picture of the expected form of the Hamiltonian vector fields. This allows one to understand their structure
a priori
and considerably simplifies the resolution of the corresponding equations. For the present paper, the geometric insight provided by Cartan geometry (see
[
22
,
23
]
) is especially useful and helpful.

The paper is organized as follows. In Section
2
, we review the aspects of Cartan geometry that provide the geometric framework and motivation for our analysis. Section
3
introduces the action that we study in the paper, discusses its symmetries and field equations, and derives several immediate consequences for the dynamics. As we will show, a number of these features are characteristic of Carrollian systems. In Section
4
, we develop the Hamiltonian formulation of the model using the GNH method. We identify the constraint structure, solve the equations defining the Hamiltonian vector fields, and establish the consistency of the resulting dynamics. Section
5
is devoted to the implementation of the time gauge, which leads to a particularly simple set of canonical variables and a simplified description of the dynamics. We conclude in Section
6
with a discussion of our results and possible directions for future work. Appendix
A
lists a number of useful mathematical facts that we use throughout the paper and summarizes our notation, and in Appendix
B
we discuss the transformations of the spacetime fields under a spacetime vector field decomposed relative to a spatial hypersurface.

2
Geometric arena

In this section, we describe Carrollian spacetimes within Cartan geometry since, as in GR, it provides the most convenient setting to work with connection-type variables.

A
(G,H)
-Cartan geometry on a smooth manifold
M
is given by an
H
-principal bundle
P
endowed with a Cartan connection
\omega\in\Omega^{1}(P,\mathfrak{g})
, i.e., a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form satisfying the following conditions (see
[
23
,
24
,
22
]
for further details):

1.

for every
p\in P
, the map
\omega_{p}:T_{p}P\to\mathfrak{g}
is a linear isomorphism;

2.

(R^{h})^{*}\omega=\mathrm{Ad}(h^{-1})\circ\omega,

for all
h\in H
, where
R^{h}
is the right action of
h
on
P
;

3.

\omega(X^{*})=X,

for all
X\in\mathfrak{h}\subset\mathfrak{g}
, where
X^{*}
is the fundamental vector field associated with
X
.

This paper focuses on the Hamiltonian formalism, therefore we will restrict ourselves to trivial (and trivialized) principal bundles

H\rightarrow P=M\times H\rightarrow M,

equipped with the canonical section

s_{e}:M\to P
,
\,s_{e}(x)=(x,e)
,
where
e
denotes the identity element of
H
. This choice allows us to pull back the Cartan connection to a
\mathfrak{g}
-valued 1-form on
M
,

W=s_{e}^{*}\omega\in\Omega^{1}(M,\mathfrak{g}).

A different choice of section,

s_{h}(x)=(x,h(x))
,
with
h:M\to H
smooth, induces the gauge transformation

W\mapsto\mathrm{Ad}(h^{-1})\circ W+h^{-1}\mathrm{d}h.

The (vertical) automorphisms of a trivial bundle are
\mathrm{Gau}(M\times H)=C^{\infty}(M,H)
.

For the purposes of this paper, it is enough to assume that
\mathfrak{g}
is reductive, namely

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{m},

which is an
\mathrm{Ad}(H)
-invariant decomposition. In particular,
W
decomposes as

W=W_{\mathfrak{h}}+W_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Under this decomposition,
W_{\mathfrak{m}}
and
W_{\mathfrak{h}}
respectively correspond to the solder form and the Ehresmann connection (pulled back to
M
).
Notice that, by the defining properties of a Cartan connection, the map

(W_{\mathfrak{m}})_{x}:T_{x}M\rightarrow\mathfrak{m}

(2.1)

is a linear isomorphism for every
x\in M
. Consequently,
M
must be parallelizable. This topological restriction is already implicit in the assumption that the principal bundle
P
is trivial. One could avoid this restriction by working with an open cover
\{U_{\alpha}\}_{\alpha}
of
M
by contractible open sets and gluing the corresponding local Cartan geometries using smooth transition functions
h_{\alpha_{1}\alpha_{2}}
. We shall not consider this more general situation here because, as discussed in the next section, all the manifolds used in the paper are parallelizable.

In what follows,
G=\mathrm{Carr}(1+3)=\mathrm{SO}(3)\ltimes\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{3}\ltimes(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R}^{3})\bigr)

is the Carroll group and
H
the closed subgroup
H\cong\mathrm{SO}(3)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
generated by the rotations and Carrollian boosts (see below).
H
is the Lie group of the principal bundle associated with the Carrollian geometry (one for each 1-form
W
) that we will discuss in the paper.

The Lie algebra
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{carr}(1+3)=\mathfrak{so}(3)_{J}\ltimes\bigl(\mathbb{R}^{3}_{C}\ltimes(\mathbb{R}_{E}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}_{P})\bigr)
, where the subscripts refer to the standard generators, is given by

\begin{array}[]{llll}[E,E]=0\,,&[E,P_{j}]=0\,,&[E,C_{j}]=0\,,&[E,J_{j}]=0\,,\\[1.29167pt]
&[P_{i},P_{j}]=0\,,&[P_{i},C_{j}]=\delta_{ij}E\,,&[P_{i},J_{j}]=\varepsilon_{ijk}P^{k}\,,\\[1.29167pt]
&&[C_{i},C_{j}]=0\,,&[C_{i},J_{j}]=\varepsilon_{ijk}C^{k}\,,\\[1.29167pt]
&&&[J_{i},J_{j}]=\varepsilon_{ijk}J^{k}\,.\end{array}

(2.2)

In practice, one can think of
X=X_{J}+X_{C}+X_{E}+X_{P}\in\mathfrak{carr}(1+3)
as a matrix:

X=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0^{t}&0\\
X_{P}^{i}b_{i}&X_{J}^{i}J_{i}&0\\
X_{E}&-X_{C}^{i}b_{i}^{t}&0\end{array}\right)

where

P_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
b_{i}&0&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad C_{i}=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
0&-b_{i}^{t}&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad J_{i}\cong\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&J_{i}&0\\
0&0&0\end{array}\right)\,,\quad E=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccc}0&0&0\\
0&0&0\\
1&0&0\end{array}\right)

and
b_{i}
is the
i
-th element of the canonical basis of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
. The subspaces

\mathfrak{h}=\mathrm{span}(J_{i},C_{i})
and
\mathfrak{m}=\mathrm{span}(E,P_{i}),

define the reductive decomposition

\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{h}\oplus\mathfrak{m}\,,\qquad\text{with}\qquad\begin{aligned} \subset\mathfrak{h}\,,\qquad&[\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{m}]\subset\mathfrak{m}\,,\\
&[\mathfrak{m},\mathfrak{m}]=0\,.\end{aligned}

Using this reductive decomposition and the standard bases in each sector, we write

W=W_{\mathfrak{h}}+W_{\mathfrak{m}},\qquad\qquad\begin{array}[]{l}W_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathsf{A}+\Upomega=\mathsf{A}^{i}J_{i}+\Upomega^{i}C_{i},\\
W_{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathsf{e}+\uptau=\mathsf{e}^{i}P_{i}+\uptau E.\end{array}

(2.3)

The Cartan curvature (see Appendix
A
for the notation)

F_{W}=\mathrm{d}W+\frac{1}{2}[W\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}W]

decomposes, projecting over
\mathfrak{h}
and
\mathfrak{m}
, as

F_{W}=(F_{W})_{\mathfrak{h}}+(F_{W})_{\mathfrak{m}}=F_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}+\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}=(F_{\mathsf{A}}+\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega)+\bigl(\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e}+\mathrm{d}\uptau+[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]\bigr)

(2.4)

The term in the second parentheses corresponds to the torsion 2-form of the solder form
W_{\mathfrak{m}}=\uptau+\mathsf{e}
:

T=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e}+\mathrm{d}\uptau+[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]

(2.5)

Notice that, if we require the torsion to vanish, we have the condition
\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}=0
, which as an equation for
W_{\mathfrak{h}}
might be incompatible. Moreover, even if a solution exists, it may not uniquely determine
\Upomega
.

The 1-parameter gauge transformations

W\mapsto\mathrm{Ad}(h_{t}^{-1})\circ W+h_{t}^{-1}\mathrm{d}h_{t}
associated with
h_{t}=\exp(tX_{\mathfrak{h}})
, where
X_{\mathfrak{h}}\in\Omega^{0}(M,\mathfrak{h})
, define the infinitesimal gauge transformation [notice that
\mathfrak{gau}(M\times H)=\Omega^{0}(M,\mathfrak{h})
]:

\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W=\mathrm{d}_{W}X_{\mathfrak{h}}=\mathrm{d}X_{\mathfrak{h}}+[W,X_{\mathfrak{h}}],

whose components are

\displaystyle\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{h}}:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W)_{\mathfrak{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}X_{\mathfrak{h}},\quad\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{m}}:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W)_{\mathfrak{m}}=-[X_{\mathfrak{h}},W_{\mathfrak{m}}].

These are the standard gauge transformations on the
H
-principal bundle, that must be gauge symmetries of our models. Typically, one considers the (non-
\mathrm{Ad}(H)
-invariant) gauge transformations of the coordinates
\{\mathsf{A},\Upomega,\mathsf{e},\uptau\}
associated with the basis
\{J,C,P,E\}
. Splitting the gauge parameter
X_{\mathfrak{h}}=X_{J}+X_{C}
and using (
2.2
):

\displaystyle\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}\mathsf{A}:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{h}})_{J}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}X_{J}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}\mathsf{e}:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{m}})_{P}=-[X_{J},\mathsf{e}]\,,

(2.6)

\displaystyle\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}\Upomega:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{h}})_{C}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}X_{C}-[X_{J},\Upomega]\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}\uptau:=(\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W_{\mathfrak{m}})_{E}=-[X_{C},\mathsf{e}]\,.

The transformation
\delta_{(X_{\mathfrak{h}})}W=\mathrm{d}_{W}X_{\mathfrak{h}}
can be
generalized
to
\delta_{(X)}W=\mathrm{d}_{W}X
for an arbitrary gauge parameter

X=X_{\mathfrak{m}}+X_{\mathfrak{h}}
,
where

X_{\mathfrak{m}}

and

X_{\mathfrak{h}}

denote the translational and vertical parts, respectively. This induces the transformations

\delta_{(X)}W_{\mathfrak{h}}:=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}X_{\mathfrak{h}}\,,\qquad\delta_{(X)}W_{\mathfrak{m}}:=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}X_{\mathfrak{m}}-[X_{\mathfrak{h}},W_{\mathfrak{m}}]\,.

(2.7)

The generalized gauge transformation admits a simple geometric interpretation. On the one hand, using Cartan’s magic formula, the Lie derivative can be rewritten as

\pounds_{\upxi}W=\mathrm{d}_{W}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W)+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}F_{W}\,.

(2.8)

On the other hand,
for any vector field
\upxi\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
, the Lie derivative of
W
splits as

\displaystyle\pounds_{\upxi}W

\displaystyle=(\pounds_{\upxi}W)_{\mathfrak{h}}+(\pounds_{\upxi}W)_{\mathfrak{m}}=\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{h}}+\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{m}}.

Therefore, using (
2.4
) and (
2.8
), these components are given by

\displaystyle\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{h}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W_{\mathfrak{h}})+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\left(F_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq: generalized delta}}{=}\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W)}W_{\mathfrak{h}}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\left(F_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}})-[(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W_{\mathfrak{h}})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}]+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\left(\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}\right)\overset{\eqref{eq: generalized delta}}{=}\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W)}W_{\mathfrak{m}}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\left(\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}\right).

When the Cartan geometry is torsion-free,
\mathrm{d}_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}W_{\mathfrak{m}}=0
, and these expressions read

\displaystyle\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{h}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W)}W_{\mathfrak{h}}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\left(F_{W_{\mathfrak{h}}}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\pounds_{\upxi}W_{\mathfrak{m}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}W)}W_{\mathfrak{m}}\,.

The previous reasoning relates generalized gauge transformations to infinitesimal diffeomorphisms of the base manifold. Explicitly, by writing a generalized gauge 0-form as
X=X_{J}+X_{C}+X_{P}+X_{E}
, we have the analog of (
2.6
) for generalized transformations:

\displaystyle\delta_{(X)}\mathsf{A}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}X_{J}\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{(X)}\mathsf{e}=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}X_{P}-[X_{J},\mathsf{e}]\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{(X)}\Upomega=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}X_{C}-[X_{J},\Upomega]\,,

\displaystyle\delta_{(X)}\uptau=\mathrm{d}X_{E}-[X_{P},\Upomega]-[X_{C},\mathsf{e}]\,.

Therefore, identifying
X_{J}=\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}
,
X_{C}=\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega
,
X_{P}=\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}
, and
X_{E}=\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau
, the action of an infinitesimal diffeomorphism generated by
\upxi
can be expressed as

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pounds_{\upxi}\mathsf{A}&=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\\
&=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})}\mathsf{A}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\,,\end{split}

(2.9a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pounds_{\upxi}\Upomega&=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)-[(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}),\Upomega]+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega)\\
&=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)}\Upomega+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega)\,,\end{split}

(2.9b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pounds_{\upxi}\mathsf{e}&=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e})-[(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}),\mathsf{e}]+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e})\\
&=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e})}\mathsf{e}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e})\,,\end{split}

(2.9c)

\displaystyle\begin{split}\pounds_{\upxi}\uptau&=\mathrm{d}(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau)-[(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega),\mathsf{e}]-[(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}),\Upomega]+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}\uptau+[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}])\\
&=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau)}\uptau+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}(\mathrm{d}\uptau+[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}])\,.\end{split}

(2.9d)

For torsion-free geometries, the last two expressions get simplified:

\pounds_{\upxi}\mathsf{e}=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e})}\mathsf{e}\,,\qquad\pounds_{\upxi}\uptau=\delta_{(\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}+\upxi\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau)}\uptau\,.

In this paper, we work with diff-invariant actions, therefore these expressions will show up in the analysis of symmetries within the Hamiltonian set up.

3
The action

Let us consider
M=[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\Sigma
a 4-dimensional manifold foliated by “spatial slices”
\Sigma_{t}:=\{t\}\times\Sigma
(
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
), where
\Sigma
is a closed, orientable, and 3-dimensional manifold (as a consequence, parallelizable). On
M
, we have a canonical evolution vector field
\partial_{\mathrm{t}}\in\mathfrak{X}(M)
, transverse to every
\Sigma_{t}
and defined by the tangent vectors to the curves
c_{p}:\mathbb{R}\rightarrow M=[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\Sigma:t\mapsto(t,p)
; and a scalar field
t\in C^{\infty}(M)
defined as
t:M\rightarrow\mathbb{R}:(\tau,p)\mapsto\tau
, such that
{\partial_{\mathrm{t}}}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathrm{d}t=1
. For each
t\in[t_{1},t_{2}]
, we define the embedding
\jmath_{t}:\Sigma\rightarrow M:p\mapsto(t,p)
. Its pullback will be denoted as
\jmath_{t}^{\ast}
. Notice that we have
\Sigma_{t}=\jmath_{t}(\Sigma)
.

Let us consider the action

S(\mathsf{A},\Upomega,\mathsf{e},\uptau)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\Big(\kappa^{-1}\big(2\uptau\wedge\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle-\langle[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{A}\Upomega\rangle\big)+\gamma^{-1}\langle[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle\Big)\,.

(3.1)

The basic fields are
\mathsf{A},\Upomega,\mathsf{e}\in\Omega^{1}(M,\mathfrak{su}(2))
and
\uptau\in\Omega^{1}(M)
, with
F_{\mathsf{A}}:=\mathrm{d}\mathsf{A}+\frac{1}{2}[\mathsf{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{A}]
and
\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega:=\mathrm{d}\Upomega+[\mathsf{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\Upomega]\,.
Here
\mathrm{d}
denotes the exterior differential in
M
and
\kappa
,
\gamma\neq 0
are real constants. Notice that
\gamma
can be interpreted as the Immirzi parameter since it comes from the Holst term.

Before we go ahead with the study of this action, some comments are in order. Although the original fields are
\mathfrak{carr}
-valued differential forms, when the basis defined in Section
2
is used to write the limit of the Holst action
[
25
]
, leaving
\uptau
aside, the resulting final expression is written in terms of
\mathbb{R}^{3}
-valued differential forms
\mathsf{e}^{i}
,
\Upomega^{i}
,
\mathsf{A}^{i}
(i.e. carrying a single “internal index”). They appear combined in expressions of the form
\mathsf{e}^{i}\wedge\Upomega^{j}\delta_{ij}
,
\epsilon_{ijk}\mathsf{e}^{i}\wedge\mathsf{e}^{j}\wedge\mathsf{A}^{k}
, etc. that can be interpreted as
\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\Upomega\rangle
,
[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]
, etc. In practice, these expressions are exactly the same as those obtained by working with fields
\mathsf{e}=\mathsf{e}^{i}J_{i}
,
\Upomega=\Upomega^{i}J_{i}
, and
\mathsf{A}=\mathsf{A}^{i}J_{i}
taking values in
\mathfrak{su}(2)
, and by considering the operations used to write the action (
3.1
). However, it is possible to map the corresponding parts of the algebra onto
\mathfrak{su}(2)
and effectively work as if all the fields used to write the action are
\mathfrak{su}(2)
-valued differential forms. Second, we want to note an interesting fact: the action (
3.1
) has the form of the (anti)self-dual action for Euclidean GR with an extra term. Specifically, if we choose the coupling constants
\kappa
and
\gamma
such that
\kappa=-\gamma
the first and third terms reproduce the self-dual action in the form appearing in
[
26
,
27
]
.

In addition to being invariant under 4-dimensional diffeomorphisms, the action (
3.1
) is invariant under two classes of local internal transformations. The first corresponds to the infinitesimal
\mathfrak{su}(2)
gauge rotations introduced in Section Section
2
[cf. Eq. (
2.6
)], which act on the fields according to

\displaystyle\delta^{1}_{(Z)}\mathsf{A}:=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}Z\,,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\delta^{1}_{(Z)}\mathsf{e}:=-[Z,\mathsf{e}]\,,

(3.2)

\displaystyle\delta^{1}_{(Z)}\Upomega:=-[Z,\Upomega]\,,

\displaystyle\delta^{1}_{(Z)}\uptau:=0\,,

where
Z\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{su}(2))
. The second class comprises the local Carrollian boosts parameterized by
y\in\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{su}(2))
:

\displaystyle\delta^{2}_{(y)}\mathsf{A}:=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta^{2}_{(y)}\mathsf{e}:=0\,,

(3.3)

\displaystyle\delta^{2}_{(y)}\Upomega:=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}y\,,\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\delta^{2}_{(y)}\uptau:=-\langle y,\mathsf{e}\rangle\,.

From a geometric perspective, the gauge transformations
\delta^{1}_{(Z)}
and
\delta^{2}_{(y)}
originate from the fundamental rotations (
J
) and Carrollian boosts (
C
) of the underlying kinematic algebra before performing the reduction to its
\mathfrak{su}(2)
sector.

As explicitly shown in Appendix
B
, these internal symmetries are deeply intertwined with spacetime geometry. When performing a
1+3
canonical splitting of the 4-dimensional manifold, the full spacetime diffeomorphisms systematically project onto the spatial hypersurface
\Sigma
as a combination of spatial diffeos and these internal gauge and boost transformations, with the effective transformation parameters matching the fields’ projections defined later in (
4.9
).

The dynamics of the theory is encoded in the following set of field equations:

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0\,,

\displaystyle 2\kappa^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\uptau\wedge\mathsf{e})-\kappa^{-1}[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]]+\gamma^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0\,,

\displaystyle\gamma^{-1}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}]-\kappa^{-1}F_{\mathsf{A}}\wedge\uptau-\kappa^{-1}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega]=0\,,

\displaystyle\kappa^{-1}\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle=0\,.

By clearing the constant coupling constants
\kappa
and
\gamma
these expressions can be simplified to:

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0\,,

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\uptau\wedge\mathsf{e})=[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]]\,,

\displaystyle[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}(\kappa F_{\mathsf{A}}-\gamma\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega)]=\gamma F_{\mathsf{A}}\wedge\uptau\,,

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle=0\,.

The first equation imposes a covariant constancy condition on the spatial soldering forms. The second equation relates the covariant derivative of the mixed
\uptau\wedge\mathsf{e}
term to a quadratic expression in
\Upomega
and
\mathsf{e}
, effectively constraining the torsion. The third equation couples the curvature
F_{\mathsf{A}}
with the covariant derivative of
\Upomega
, mixing the isotropy and translational sectors. Finally, the fourth equation provides an additional algebraic constraint on the curvature.

If in (
3.4
) we take the limit
\kappa\rightarrow\infty
, we recover the field equations for the HK model
[
28
]
:

\displaystyle[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}]=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0\,.

These equations describe a theory related to GR with simple dynamics and a Hamiltonian formulation in terms of Ashtekar variables.

If, instead, we take the limit
\gamma\rightarrow\infty
, the field equations become

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0\,,

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\uptau\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e})-[\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]]=0\,,

\displaystyle F_{\mathsf{A}}\wedge\uptau+[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega]=0\,,

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle=0\,.

These are the equations given by the Einstein-Cartan action.

It is worth noting that the field equations for Euclidean GR in terms of the fields used here can be written in the form

\displaystyle[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}]=-F_{\mathsf{A}}\wedge\uptau\,,

\displaystyle 2\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\uptau\wedge\mathsf{e})=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]\,,

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle=0\,.

where the terms that appear in the field equations coming from the action (
3.1
) are mixed in interesting ways.

In order to facilitate the comparison of our results with the literature on the Hamiltonian treatment of gravitational actions similar to the one discussed here, we will take
\kappa=1
but keep the Immirzi parameter
\gamma
in the rest of the paper.
As shown in
A.3
, the equations (
3.5
) can be simplified to

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e}=0\,,

(3.9a)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\uptau+\langle\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}\rangle=0\,,

\displaystyle F_{\mathsf{A}}\wedge\uptau+[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega]=0\,,

\displaystyle\langle\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}\rangle=0\,.

Notice that the dependence on
\gamma
drops out. This phenomenon is similar to the one that happens with the Holst action. As in that case, the term of the action controlled by the Immirzi parameter does not change the field equations.

The system that we study in the paper admits an interesting geometric interpretation. From equation (
2.5
), it is clear that equations (
3.9a
) and (
3.9
) are equivalent to the vanishing of the torsion (a condition that ties together the translational and isotropy sectors). In particular, the first equation imposes the covariant constancy of the spatial coframe
\mathsf{e}
, while the second relates the covariant derivative of the clock form
\uptau
to a contraction involving
\Upomega
and
\mathsf{e}

[
29
]
. The remaining equations (
3.9
) and (
3.9
) govern the curvature dynamics.

Although the main purpose of the paper is to study the Hamiltonian formulation for the action (
3.1
), there is interesting information that can be gleaned from the field equations. By introducing a foliation of the spacetime manifold
M
(or using the one available by construction) and defining appropriate projected geometric objects in terms of
\{\mathsf{A},\Upomega,\mathsf{e},\uptau\}
, it is possible to get a rather clear idea about how the Hamiltonian formulation will look like, both as far as the constraints and the equations of motion are concerned. In the rest of the section we will look at a specific feature of the dynamics given by (
3.5
) connected to a concrete description of the collapsed Carrollian light cones.

Let us restrict ourselves to solutions to the field equations such that
(\mathsf{e},\uptau)
defines a (non-degenerate) coframe. We will then have a dual frame
(\mathsf{u},\mathsf{E}_{i})
such that
\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau=1
,
\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}=0
,
\mathsf{E}_{i}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\uptau=0
and
\mathsf{E}_{i}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{e}^{j}=\delta^{j}_{i}
. From Lemma
A.3
and
[F_{\mathsf{A}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}]=0
(which follows from
\mathrm{d}_{A}\mathsf{e}=0
), we have
\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\mathsf{e}=0
and
\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}F_{\mathsf{A}}=0
. We can then use (
2.9
) to obtain the Lie derivatives along
\mathsf{u}
of solutions
\{\mathsf{A},\Upomega,\mathsf{e},\uptau\}
to the field equations (
3.5
):

\displaystyle\pounds_{\mathsf{u}}\mathsf{A}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})\,,

\displaystyle\pounds_{\mathsf{u}}\Upomega

\displaystyle=\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)-[(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\Upomega]-(*_{\mathsf{e}}F_{\mathsf{A}})^{\top}\,,

\displaystyle\pounds_{\mathsf{u}}\mathsf{e}\,

\displaystyle=-[(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e})]\,,

\displaystyle\pounds_{\mathsf{u}}\uptau\,

\displaystyle=-\langle(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}\rangle\,.

In the last equation, we have used

whereas, in the second one, we have used
[\mathsf{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathrm{d}_{\mathsf{A}}\Upomega)]=F_{\mathsf{A}}

\mathsf{X}=\mathchoice{\mathsf{X}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{j}}}_{{{i}\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}}\mathsf{e}^{i}J_{j}
, we define its “transpose” as
\mathsf{X}^{\top}=\mathchoice{\mathsf{X}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}{\mathsf{X}^{{{j}\mathchoice{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[2.82928pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[1.68811pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.2058pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}}}_{{\mathchoice{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\displaystyle$}}{\makebox[3.71356pt][c]{$\textstyle$}}{\makebox[2.29834pt][c]{$\scriptstyle$}}{\makebox[1.64166pt][c]{$\scriptscriptstyle$}}{i}}}}\mathsf{e}^{i}J_{j}
.

As we can see, these expressions give a very neat picture of the dynamics of magnetic Carrollian gravity
[
6
]
. With the exception of
\pounds_{\mathsf{u}}\Upomega
, where a contribution
-(*_{\mathsf{e}}F_{\mathsf{A}})^{\top}
is present, the rest of the Lie derivatives along solutions to the field equations can be interpreted as either internal
SU(2)
rotations with parameter
(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathsf{A})
or Carrollian boosts with parameter
(\mathsf{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)
. In view of this result, the integral curves of
\mathsf{u}
can be interpreted as the collapsed light cones.

We end this section by noting that the field equations do not imply the integrability of the distribution

\mathcal{T}=\{v\in TM\,:\,\uptau(v)=0\}.

Indeed, if we decompose

\Upomega=\uptau\wedge(\mathrm{u}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Upomega)+\underline{\Upomega},

and demand the integrability of
\mathcal{T}
:

0=\uptau\wedge\mathrm{d}\uptau\overset{\eqref{EOM_2}}{=}-\uptau\wedge\langle\Upomega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}\rangle,

we get the additional condition

\langle\underline{\Upomega}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathsf{e}\rangle=0.

We will recover an analog of this in Section
5
, when we discuss the time gauge in the Hamiltonian formulation.

4
The Hamiltonian formulation

We now obtain the Hamiltonian formulation starting from the Lagrangian. To this end we take advantage of the fact that the spacetime manifold
M
has the form
[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\Sigma
and use the scalar field
t\in C^{\infty}(M)
and the vector field
\partial_{\mathrm{t}}
canonically associated with
M
. The action can be written as the integral of a 4-form
\mathcal{L}
over
M

\int_{[t_{1},t_{2}]\times\Sigma}\mathcal{L}=\int_{t_{1}}^{t_{2}}\mathrm{d}t\int_{\Sigma}\jmath_{t}^{*}\partial_{\mathrm{t}}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathcal{L}\,.

From this expression, we can read the Lagrangian
L:=\int_{\Sigma}\jmath_{t}^{*}\partial_{\mathrm{t}}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathcal{L}
. This is a real function on the tangent bundle of the configuration space [here
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{su}(2)
]

Q\subset V:=(\Omega^{0}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g})\times\Omega^{1}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g}))^{3}\times(\Omega^{0}(\Sigma)\times\Omega^{1}(\Sigma))\,.

given by the elements of the form
q:=(e_{\mathrm{t}},e,A_{\mathrm{t}},A,\Omega_{\mathrm{t}},\Omega,\tau_{\mathrm{t}},\tau)
subject to the non-degeneracy condition for
\{e_{\mathrm{t}},e,\tau_{\mathrm{t}},\tau\}
discussed before [see equations (
2.1
) and (
2.3
)]. Since these restrictions can be expressed as inequalities, they define an open (and in fact dense) subset, thereby preserving the local dimensionality. The velocities have the form
\mathrm{v}_{q}:=(v_{e_{\mathrm{t}}},v_{e},v_{A_{\mathrm{t}}},v_{A},v_{\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}},v_{\Omega},v_{\tau_{\mathrm{t}}},v_{\tau})
.

By computing
\jmath_{t}^{*}\partial_{\mathrm{t}}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\mathcal{L}
from the action (
3.1
) we obtain the Lagrangian

\displaystyle\begin{split}L(\mathrm{v}_{q})&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\,\,\langle v_{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}(\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]-2e\wedge\tau)\rangle-\langle v_{\Omega}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\rangle\\
&\hskip 36.98866pt+\langle 2e_{\mathrm{t}},(\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{A}]-[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{A}\Omega]-(F_{A}\wedge\tau))\rangle\phantom{\Big|}\\
&\hskip 36.98866pt+\langle A_{\mathrm{t}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}(\gamma^{-1}\mathrm{d}_{A}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]-[\Omega\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]]-2\mathrm{d}_{A}(e\wedge\tau))\rangle\\
&\hskip 36.98866pt-\langle\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{A}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\rangle+2\tau_{\mathrm{t}}\wedge\langle e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{A}\rangle\Big)\,.\end{split}

(4.1)

Here we have introduced the curvature

F_{A}:=\mathrm{d}A+\frac{1}{2}[A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}A]
, and the covariant exterior differential
\mathrm{d}_{A}
which acts on
\alpha\in\Omega^{k}(\Sigma,\mathfrak{g})
as
\mathrm{d}_{A}\alpha:=\mathrm{d}\alpha+[A\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\alpha]\,.

The fiber derivative of the Lagrangian
FL:TQ\rightarrow T^{*}Q
is given by

FL(\mathrm{v}_{q})(\mathrm{w}_{q})=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\left(\langle w_{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}(\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]-2e\wedge\tau)\rangle-\langle w_{\Omega}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\rangle\right)\,.

This provides the definition of momenta. The non-zero components are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\,\,{\mathrm{p}}_{A}&=\frac{1}{2}(\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]-2(e\wedge\tau))\\
{\mathrm{p}}_{\Omega}&=-\frac{1}{2}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\end{split}

(4.2)

where we consider the obvious pairing to define their action on vectors. Since no velocities are involved, all the momenta can be removed and the primary constraint submanifold
\mathsf{M}_{0}
is diffeomorphic to
Q
. In particular, the components of the momenta of a generic vector field
\mathbb{Y}
in phase space can be rewritten in terms of the components of the configuration variables.

The non-vanishing components of the vector fields tangent to the primary constraint submanifold are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\mathrm{Y}}_{\!\mathrm{p}A}=\gamma^{-1}[Y_{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]-\,Y_{e}\wedge\tau-\,e\wedge Y_{\tau}\,,\\
&{\mathrm{Y}}_{\!\mathrm{p}\Omega}=-[Y_{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\,,\end{split}

(4.3)

Therefore, the canonical symplectic form pulled back to
\mathsf{M}_{0}
reads

\displaystyle\begin{split}\omega_{0}(\mathbb{X}_{0},\mathbb{Y}_{0})=&\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\langle Y_{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\big(\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}X_{A}]-\,\tau\wedge X_{A}-[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}X_{\Omega}]\big)\rangle\\
&\hskip 11.38109pt+\langle Y_{A}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\big(X_{e}\wedge\tau+\,e\wedge X_{\tau}-\gamma^{-1}[X_{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\big)\rangle\\
&\hskip 11.38109pt+\langle Y_{\Omega}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}[X_{e}\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}e]\rangle\\
&\hskip 11.38109pt+Y_{\tau}\wedge\langle e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}X_{A}\rangle\Big)\,.\end{split}

(4.4)

for a generic field
\mathbb{X}_{0}=(X_{e_{\mathrm{t}}},\!X_{e},X_{A_{\mathrm{t}}},\!X_{A},\!X_{\Omega_{\mathrm{t}}},\!X_{\Omega},\!X_{\tau_{\mathrm{t}}},\!X_{\tau})
and analogously for
\mathbb{Y}_{0}
.

Meanwhile, since the energy
E(v)=FL(v)(v)-L(v)
only depends on the positions, it is equal to the Hamiltonian which is given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}H(q)&=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Sigma}\Big(\langle 2e_{\mathrm{t}},([e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}\mathrm{d}_{A}\Omega]+(F_{A}\wedge\tau)-\gamma^{-1}[e\mathbin{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\displaystyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\textstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr$\scriptscriptstyle\wedge$\cr\smash{\raisebox{-0.86108pt}{$\scriptscriptstyle\cdot$}}\cr}}}}F_{A}])\rangle\phantom{\Big|}\\

(4.5)

The main equation that has to be solved in the GNH setting is
(\mathbb{X}\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\!\!\!\!\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\Omega-\mathrm{d\!\raisebox{-0.05pt}{\scalebox{.8}[1.0]{I}}}H)|{\mathsf{M}_{0}}=0
(here
\mathrm{d\!\raisebox{-0.05pt}{\scalebox{.8}[1.0]{I}}}
and
\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}\!\!\!\!\mathbin{\smash{\raisebox{8.0pt}{\scalebox{1}[-2.5]{$\neg\,$}}}}
respectively denote the exterior and interior differentials in phase space). To this end, we compute
\mathrm{d\!\raisebox{-0.05pt}{\scalebox{.8}[1.0]{I}}}H(\mathbb{Y}_{0})
for an arbitrary vector field
\mathbb{Y}_{0}
tangent to
\mathsf{M}_{0}
and compare it to (

). This leads to the following
